[2025:RJ-JP:32553-DB] (128 of 131) [SAW-612/2024]
For Appellant(s) : Mr. Himanshu Jain, Advocate.
Mr. Ghanshyam Sharma, Advocate.
Mr. Kapil Kumar Khandelwal,
Advocate.
Mr. Harendar Neel, Advocate.
Mr. Raghu Nandan Sharma, Advocate.
For Respondent(s) : Mr. Nalin G. Narain, Advocate.
Mr. Ram Pratap Saini, Advocate.
Mr. Sanjeev Kumar Singhal.
Ms. Komal Kumari Giri, Advocate.
Mr. Aamir Khan, Advocate.
Mr. Abhijeet Panchariya, Advocate.
HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE INDERJEET SINGH
 HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE BHUWAN GOYAL
Order
19/08/2025
1. All the appellants are aggrieved by the order dated
28.11.2023 passed by learned Single Judge wherein in paragraph
11 of the judgment, it has been held has under:
It has been brought to the notice of this
Court that the RSSB has already made
recommendation to the State
Government for giving appointment to
the selected candidates on the post of
Teacher Grade-III Level-II in various
subjects. The respondent- State shall
make a mention in the appointment
orders to be issued about the order of re-examination of the answer key and in
case the result is revised and any person
who has also been given appointment
prior to the revision of the result, does
not find place in the revised merit list,
the appointment given to him before
revised result will not create any right in
his favour to continue.
2. Grievance of the appellants is that certain observations have
been made against the appellants in paragraph 11 of the order
dated 28.11.2023 passed by the learned Single Judge whereas
none of the appellants was party to the writ petition. Counsels

[2025:RJ-JP:32553-DB] (129 of 131) [SAW-612/2024]
further submit that all the appellants have already been appointed
and joined the post of Teacher Grade-III Level-II in various
subjects in pursuance of the advertisement dated 16.12.2022.
Counsel further submits that if the respondents want to revise the
result in compliance with any order of this Court or any order of
the Principal Seat at Jodhpur, their services may not be
terminated.
3. Counsels relied upon the judgment passed by the Hon’ble
Supreme Court in the matter of “Girjesh Shrivastava and Ors.
versus State of M.P. and Ors.” 2010 (6) ALLMR (SC) 930
wherein in paragraph 28, it has been held as under:
28. More importantly, in deciding these
issues, the High Court should have been
mindful of the fact that an order for
cancellation of appointment would render
most of the appellants unemployed. Most of
them were earlier teaching in non-formal
education centres, from where they had
resigned to apply in response to the
advertisement. They had left their previous
employment in view of the fact that for their
three-year long teaching experiences, the
interview process in the present selection was
awarding them grace marks of 25%. It had
also given them a relaxation of 8 years with
respect to their age. Now, if they lose their
jobs as a result of the High Court's order, they
would be effectively unemployed as they
cannot even revert to their earlier jobs in the
non-formal education centres, which have
been abolished since then. This would
severely affect the economic security of many
families. Most of them are between the age
group of 35-45 years, and the prospects for
them of finding another job are rather dim.
Some of them were in fact awaiting their
salary raise at the time of quashing of their
appointment by the High Court.”
4. Counsels relied upon the judgment passed by the Hon’ble
Supreme Court in the matter of “Anmol Kumar Tiwari and Ors.
versus The State of Jharkhand and Ors”. Versus The State of

[2025:RJ-JP:32553-DB] (130 of 131) [SAW-612/2024]
Jharkhand and Ors.” AIR 2021 SC 1139 wherein in paragraph 09,
it has been held as under:
9. Two issues arise for our
consideration. The first relates to the
correctness of the direction given by the
High Court to reinstate the Writ
Petitioners. The High Court directed
reinstatement of the Writ Petitioners after
taking into account the fact that they
were beneficiaries of the select list that
was prepared in an irregular manner.
However, the High Court found that the
Writ Petitioners were not responsible for
the irregularities committed by the
authorities in preparation of the select
list. Moreover, the Writ Petitioners were
appointed after completion of training and
worked for some time. The High Court
was of the opinion that the Writ
Petitioners ought to be considered for
reinstatement without affecting the rights
of other candidates who were already
selected. A similar situation arose in Vikas
Pratap Singh’s case (supra), where this
Court considered that the Appellants-therein were appointed due to an error
committed by the Respondents in the
matter of valuation of answer scripts. As
there was no allegation of fraud or
misrepresentation committed by the
Appellants therein, the termination of
their services was set aside as it would
adversely affect their careers. That the
Appellants-therein had successfully
undergone training and were serving the
State for more than 3 years was another
reason that was given by this Court for
setting aside the orders passed by the
High Court. As the Writ Petitioners are
similarly situated to the Appellants in
Vikas Pratap Singh’s case (supra), we are
in agreement with the High Court that the
Writ Petitioners are entitled to the relief
granted. Moreover, though on pain of
Contempt, the Writ Petitioners have been
reinstated and are working at present.
5. Counsels for the respondents have not disputed this fact that
the appellants were not impleaded as party before the learned
Single Judge.
6. Heard counsels for the parties and perused the record.

[2025:RJ-JP:32553-DB] (131 of 131) [SAW-612/2024]
7. Admittedly, the learned Single Judge has made certain
observations with regard to appointment of the appellants in first
round.
8. We are of the considered view that even if after revision of
result respondents would appoint successful candidates, then also
services of appellants cannot be terminated by the State because
there was no allegation of fraud or misrepresentation committed
by the appellants herein in connection with their appointment.
9. In that view of the matter, these appeals are allowed and
paragraph 11 of the order dated 28.11.2023 passed by the
learned Single Judge is set aside. The respondents are further
directed not to terminate the services of the appellants herein in
view of the judgments passed by the Hon’ble Supreme Court in
the matter of Girjesh Shrivastava and Anmol Kumar Tiwari
(supra).
(BHUWAN GOYAL),J (INDERJEET SINGH),J
Tushar/95-146, 218 and 224
